```
# BOOTSTRAP — read this file FIRST

You are resuming work on **carry**: a C/C++ project manager in the spirit of Rust's Cargo,
written in Rust, driving **CMake + Conan 2 + Ninja + g++** underneath.

Your context was cleared. **This repository is your memory.** Do not rely on conversation history.

## Session protocol (always, in this exact order)

1. Read `STATE.md` — current task, one-line state, environment. (This is the only file you must read in full every session.)
2. Read `TASKS.md` — the backlog and status table.
3. Read the task file for the current task: `tasks/Txxx-<slug>.md`. It is self-contained: goal, context, steps, acceptance criteria.
4. Skim the `## Key decisions` and `## Open issues` sections of `results/Tyyy.md` for each task it depends on.
5. Do the work. Run every acceptance-criteria command and note the real output.
6. When done, **record** (this is mandatory — the next session knows nothing otherwise):
   - Write `results/Txxx.md`: what was implemented, key decisions, verification (commands + last lines of output), open issues, hints for follow-up tasks.
   - Update the task's status in `TASKS.md`.
   - Rewrite `STATE.md` (next task, one-line state). Keep it under ~60 lines.
   - Append a one-paragraph entry (newest on top) to `SESSION_LOG.md`.
   - New bugs/regressions → `ISSUES.md` (do not silently fix issues outside the current task).
7. **One session = at most one task**, unless the task is trivially small. If blocked: say why in `STATE.md`, file the blocker in `ISSUES.md`, and stop.

## Hard rules

- Never silently rewrite `DESIGN.md`. Architecture changes go into its `## Decisions` section with a date, and a line in `SESSION_LOG.md`.
- Verification output in results: paste the command + last few lines, never whole logs.
- In carry-managed projects, `conanfile.txt` and `CMakeLists.txt` are **generated by carry** — never hand-edit them in examples; fix the generator instead.
- `CARRY.toml` is user-owned; edits must preserve it as valid TOML (use `toml_edit`).
- Do not install tools or packages system-wide. Use `cargo add` for Rust deps.
- If you are unsure whether a file is yours to touch: it is not. Ask or stop.
```

```markdown
Implement the `CARRY.toml` manifest model and the project scaffolders, so that
`carry new demo` produces a complete, valid project directory (not yet buildable — the
generated files come in T003, but the scaffold includes placeholder `conanfile.txt` and
`CMakeLists.txt` stubs that T003 will replace with real rendering).

## Context

- Read `DESIGN.md` §3 (project layout), §4 (schema), and `results/T001.md` (module layout as built).
- Add deps with `cargo add`: `serde` (feature `derive`), `toml`, `toml_edit`.
- Work in `src/manifest.rs` (schema + IO) and `src/template.rs` (scaffold file contents).

## Requirements

### manifest.rs
- `#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize)]` types: `Manifest { package: Package, build: Build, dependencies: BTreeMap<String, String> }` with serde defaults so partial TOML parses (defaults: version "0.1.0", type "bin", cpp_std "c++23", c_std "c11"). Use `#[serde(default)]` per field; `dependencies` defaults to empty map.
- `Manifest::load(path) -> anyhow::Result<Self>` and `Manifest::save(&self, path)` — save via `toml_edit` (parse existing doc, set values, write back) so user comments/format survive when T006 edits deps. For now, `save` may start from the parsed existing file if present, else from `toml::to_string_pretty`.
- Validation: `name` must match `[a-z0-9_][a-z0-9_-]*`; `type` ∈ {bin, lib, both}; `version` non-empty. Return `anyhow` errors with `help:` hints.
- `fn conan_ref(pkg: &str, version: &str) -> String` — the mapping from DESIGN.md §4:
  plain → `pkg/ver`, starts with `>` `<` `=` `^` `~` → `pkg/[range]`, empty → `pkg/*`.
- Unit tests (in-file `#[cfg(test)]`): parse a full manifest, parse a minimal one (only
  `[package] name`), round-trip save→load, name validation failures, all `conan_ref` branches.

### template.rs + new/init
- `cmd_new(name, lib: bool)` in `main.rs` (or a `scaffold.rs`-style function set in template.rs):
  - Validate `name` (same rule); refuse to create inside an existing non-empty dir without `CARRY.toml` (error: "dir exists and is not a carry project").
```

````markdown
# carry — Design Document

**carry** is a C/C++ project manager that behaves like Rust's **Cargo**: a single small CLI,
a project manifest, and deterministic build/test/run commands. Under the hood it drives
**CMake** (configuration), **Conan 2** (dependencies), **Ninja** (build), and **g++** (compiler).

This document is the stable reference. **Do not rewrite silently** — architecture changes are
appended to `## Decisions` with a date.

---

## 1. Vision

A C/C++ developer should be able to:

```
carry new myapp          # scaffold
carry add fmt            # add a dependency
carry build --release    # compile
carry run                # build + run
carry test               # build + run tests
carry clean              # wipe build/
```

…without ever touching CMakeLists.txt or conanfile.txt by hand. The manifest (`CARRY.toml`)
is the single source of truth; everything else is generated.

## 2. Command surface (MVP)

| Command | Behavior |
|---|---|
| `carry new <name> [--lib]` | Create `./<name>/` scaffold (bin by default; `--lib` adds a library target) |
| `carry init` | Turn the current dir into a carry project (name from dir; errors if CARRY.toml exists) |
| `carry build [--release]` | Regenerate → conan install → cmake configure → ninja build. Default Debug, `--release` = Release |
| `carry run [args]...` | `build` (Debug) then exec `build/bin/<name> [args]...` |
| `carry test` | `build` (Debug) then `ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure` |
| `carry clean` | Remove `build/` |
| `carry add <pkg>[@<version>]` | Add/update a dependency in CARRY.toml (toml_edit, preserves formatting) |
| `carry remove <pkg>` | Remove a dependency from CARRY.toml |
| `carry --version`, `--help` | clap-provided |

Non-commands for MVP (future): workspace, publish, lockfile management, custom cmake hooks,
cross-compilation, MSVC/Clang.

## 3. Generated project layout

```
<project>/
├── CARRY.toml          # manifest — user-owned, single source of truth
├── conanfile.txt       # generated by carry
├── CMakeLists.txt      # generated by carry
├── .gitignore          # written by `carry new` (contains build/)
├── src/
│   ├── main.cpp        # if type includes bin
│   └── lib.cpp         # if type includes lib
├── include/<name>/
│   └── <name>.hpp      # if type includes lib
└── tests/
    └── test_main.cpp   # trivial assert-based test binary (no framework), always created
```

Conventions:
- Build dir: `build/` at project root (gitignored). Binaries land in `build/bin/<name>`.
- Library target name: `<name>_lib` (avoids clashing with the executable name).
- Test target: `test_<name>`, registered with CTest as `<name>_tests`.
- Dependency CMake target: `<pkg>::<pkg>` (Conan 2 default); exotic packages needing a custom
  mapping are future work (explicit override key in the manifest).

## 4. CARRY.toml schema

```toml
[package]
name = "demo"          # required; [a-z0-9_][a-z0-9_-]*
version = "0.1.0"      # required; semver string
type = "bin"           # "bin" | "lib" | "both"   (default "bin")

[build]
cpp_std = "c++23"      # default "c++23"
c_std   = "c11"        # default "c11"

[dependencies]
fmt = "10.2.1"         # pkg = version ("10.2.1") or range (">=10")
```

Version mapping to Conan refs:
- plain version `10.2.1` → `fmt/10.2.1`
- range `>=10` → `fmt/[>=10]`
- bare `fmt` (no version) → `fmt/*`

## 5. Generated files

### conanfile.txt (generated)

```
[requires]
fmt/10.2.1

[generators]
CMakeDeps
CMakeToolchain
```

### CMakeLists.txt (generated)

```cmake
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.21)
project(demo VERSION 0.1.0 LANGUAGES C CXX)

set(CMAKE_C_STANDARD 11)
set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 23)
set(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)
set(CMAKE_RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY ${CMAKE_BINARY_DIR}/bin)

find_package(fmt REQUIRED)          # one per dependency

add_library(demo_lib STATIC src/lib.cpp)     # only if lib
target_include_directories(demo_lib PUBLIC include)

add_executable(demo src/main.cpp)            # only if bin
target_link_libraries(demo PRIVATE demo_lib fmt::fmt)   # lib first, then deps
target_include_directories(demo PRIVATE include)

enable_testing()
add_executable(test_demo tests/test_main.cpp)
target_link_libraries(test_demo PRIVATE demo fmt::fmt)
add_test(NAME demo_tests COMMAND test_demo)
```

Regeneration rule: on every `build`/`run`/`test`, carry renders both files in memory and
rewrites them **iff content differs** from what is on disk. No state file needed.

## 6. Build pipeline (`carry build`)

Run from the project root (carry walks up from cwd to find `CARRY.toml`; error if none).

1. Parse `CARRY.toml` → `Manifest` (serde).
2. Render `conanfile.txt` + `CMakeLists.txt`; write if changed.
3. `conan install . --output-folder=build --build=missing -s build_type=<Debug|Release>`
   → writes `build/conan_toolchain.cmake` + CMakeDeps configs into `build/`.
4. `cmake -S . -B build -G Ninja \
      -DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE=build/conan_toolchain.cmake \
      -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=<Debug|Release> \
      -DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=gcc -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=g++`
   Always run (reconfigure is cheap; keeps conan updates in sync).
5. `cmake --build build`

`carry test` adds: `ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure`
`carry run` adds: exec `build/bin/<name> <args...>`, propagating exit code.

Tool invocation rules:
- Stream child stdout/stderr directly to the terminal (never buffer build output).
- On failure: print `error: <tool> failed with exit N` and the command that was run; exit non-zero.
- Missing tool (not on PATH): `error: required tool 'conan' not found on PATH`.
- `--verbose` (global flag): print each command before running it.

## 7. Rust implementation structure

```
Cargo.toml          # bin "carry"; deps: clap(derive), serde(derive), toml, toml_edit, anyhow
src/
├── main.rs         # clap CLI, command dispatch, exit codes, --verbose
├── manifest.rs     # Manifest struct (serde), load/save via toml_edit, version-ref mapping
├── project.rs      # find_project_root() (walk up for CARRY.toml), path helpers
├── template.rs     # `carry new`/`init` scaffold contents (main.cpp, lib.cpp, hpp, test_main.cpp, .gitignore)
├── generate.rs     # Manifest → conanfile.txt / CMakeLists.txt render + write-if-changed
└── build.rs        # run() helper, build()/test()/run()/clean() pipelines
tests/
├── manifest_test.rs   # parse round-trips, version mapping, validation
├── generate_test.rs   # rendered file contents, idempotence (write-if-changed)
└── e2e.rs             # spawn the built binary: new→build→run→test→add→clean in a temp dir
```

Testing policy:
- Unit tests for manifest + generator (fast, no toolchain needed).
- Integration tests that shell out to the real `carry` binary (env `CARRY_BIN` or cargo target path)
  in `tempfile` dirs. E2E tests requiring Conan Center network are marked `#[ignore]`
  and listed in T007.

## 8. Error handling & UX

- `anyhow::Result` end-to-end; `#[derive(Parser)]` clap with subcommands.
- Errors prefixed `error: `; usage hints after `help: ` when obvious.
- Exit codes: 0 ok; 1 carry error; 101 propagated child (build/test/run) exit code for run.
- No colors in MVP (plain text, grep-friendly).

## 9. Non-goals (MVP)

No lockfile (Conan's own cache resolution is enough for now), no registry/publish, no
workspace/multi-package, no custom-CMake escape hatch, no non-GCC compilers, no Windows paths,
no Conan profile management (use the `default` profile).

## 10. Future work (post-MVP, ordered)

1. `conan lock` support for reproducible builds (`carry freeze` / `carry update`).
2. `extra.cmake` hook: optional user file `include()`d at the end of generated CMakeLists.
3. Dependency target-name override (`[dependencies.fmt] target = "..."` table form).
4. Colors + `--quiet`.
5. `carry update` (refresh versions), `carry metadata` (print resolved graph).
6. MSVC/Clang via `cc`/`cxx` keys in `[build]`.

## Decisions

- **D1 (2026-09-17)** — Implement carry in **Rust**: user is Rust-fluent, cargo is available,
  fast testable CLI, `toml_edit` for manifest editing. The *managed* projects are C/C++.
- **D2 (2026-09-17)** — **Conan 2 with `conanfile.txt`** (not `.py`): text format is trivial to
  generate and diff; script logic not needed for MVP.
- **D3 (2026-09-17)** — **Manifest is the single source of truth**: carry regenerates
  `conanfile.txt` and `CMakeLists.txt` on every build (write-if-changed). Users do not edit
  generated files; future `extra.cmake` hook for escape hatches.
- **D4 (2026-09-17)** — Build dir is `build/`; **Debug is the default** (fastest feedback,
  matches CMake convention); `--release` opt-in.
- **D5 (2026-09-17)** — Configure with `-DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE=build/conan_toolchain.cmake`
  (CMakeToolchain sets prefix/module paths itself — no manual `CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH`).
- **D6 (2026-09-17)** — Pin `CMAKE_C_COMPILER=gcc` / `CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=g++` explicitly
  (deterministic compiler, matches the user's toolchain).
- **D7 (2026-09-17)** — **Repo-as-memory**: `BOOTSTRAP.md` (protocol) + `STATE.md` (current
  state) + `TASKS.md` (backlog) + `tasks/` (self-contained task prompts) + `results/`
  (per-task outcomes) + `ISSUES.md` + `SESSION_LOG.md` + `prompts/` (paste-ready session
  prompts). Every session follows the protocol in BOOTSTRAP.md and ends by recording.
- **D8 (2026-09-17)** — E2E tests needing Conan Center network are `#[ignore]`d so the
  default test suite is hermetic; T007 exercises them manually and records the result.
````

```rust
//! carry — a C/C++ project manager in the spirit of Cargo.
//!
//! T001: CLI scaffold. All MVP subcommands from DESIGN.md §2 are defined; each
//! command is a stub returning "not implemented" until its owning task lands
//! (new/init → T002, build → T004, run/test/clean → T005, add/remove → T006).

// Module files are empty scaffolds until their owning tasks fill them in;
// remove the allow on each as it becomes used (T002–T005).
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod build;
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod generate;
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod manifest;
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod project;
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod template;

use clap::{Parser, Subcommand};

#[derive(Parser)]
#[command(name = "carry", version, about = "A C/C++ project manager in the spirit of Cargo")]
struct Cli {
    /// Print each external command before running it (wired up in T004)
    #[arg(long, global = true)]
    #[allow(dead_code)]
    verbose: bool,

    #[command(subcommand)]
    command: Commands,
}

#[derive(Subcommand)]
enum Commands {
    /// Create a new carry project at ./<name>/
    New {
        /// Project name
        name: String,

        /// Also add a library target
        #[arg(long)]
        lib: bool,
    },

    /// Turn the current directory into a carry project
    Init,

    /// Regenerate files, install dependencies, configure, and build
    Build {
        /// Build with the Release build type (default: Debug)
        #[arg(long)]
        release: bool,
    },

    /// Build (Debug) then run the project binary with the given arguments
    Run {
        #[arg(trailing_var_arg = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
        args: Vec<String>,
    },

    /// Build (Debug) then run the test suite
    Test,

    /// Remove the build/ directory
    Clean,

    /// Add or update a dependency (e.g. `fmt` or `fmt/10.2.1`)
    Add {
        /// Package spec
        pkg: String,
    },

    /// Remove a dependency
    Remove {
        /// Package name
        pkg: String,
    },
}

fn main() {
    let cli = Cli::parse();
    let not_implemented = |cmd: &str, planned: &str| -> ! {
        eprintln!("error: {cmd} not implemented yet (planned in {planned})");
        std::process::exit(1);
    };
    match cli.command {
        Commands::New { .. } => not_implemented("new", "T002"),
        Commands::Init => not_implemented("init", "T002"),
        Commands::Build { .. } => not_implemented("build", "T004"),
        Commands::Run { .. } => not_implemented("run", "T005"),
        Commands::Test => not_implemented("test", "T005"),
        Commands::Clean => not_implemented("clean", "T005"),
        Commands::Add { .. } => not_implemented("add", "T006"),
        Commands::Remove { .. } => not_implemented("remove", "T006"),
    }
}

```

Now the manifest struct details:

```rust
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::Path;

use anyhow::Context;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

/// A CARRY.toml manifest — single source of truth for a carry project.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Manifest {
    #[serde(default)]
    pub package: Package,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub build: Build,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub dependencies: BTreeMap<String, String>,
}
```

Wait — `#[serde(default)]` on a struct field requires the field's type to implement Default. Package and Build need `impl Default` — either derive with `#[derive(Default)]` (name defaults to "" which then fails validation — good) or hand-implement. `#[derive(Default)]` works: name "", version... no wait, version must default to "0.1.0" — derive(Default) would give "". So hand-impl Default:
```rust
impl Default for Package {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self { name: String::new(), version: "0.1.0".into(), kind: "bin".into() }
    }
}
```
And for the field: `#[serde(default)] pub package: Package`.

Package:
```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Package {
    pub name: String,
    #[serde(default = "default_version")]
    pub version: String,
    #[serde(rename = "type", default = "default_type")]
    pub kind: String,
}
```
Or simpler: `#[serde(default)]` on Package fields with hand-implied default values... a `#[serde(default)]` on a field without a named function uses `Default::default()` for the field type — String → "". So version would be "" not "0.1.0". Hence named default functions or hand-implied Default on the whole struct.

Cleanest: `#[serde(default)]` on the struct-level fields `package`/`build`, and impl Default for Package/Build with the required defaults. For individual fields, if a TOML has `[package]` with only name, version would be missing → needs a field-level default too! Since `[package]` is present but `version` is absent, serde deserializes Package with missing version field → needs `#[serde(default = ...)]` on the version field.

So:
```rust
pub struct Package {
    pub name: String,  // no default: missing [package] → Package::default() (name=""), then validate fails with clear error. But [package] present without name? Missing field without default → serde error "missing field `name`". Hmm — is that OK? The requirement says "serde defaults so partial TOML parses (defaults: version..., type...)". name isn't in the defaults list → name is required. Serde's "missing field name" error is fine. But wait: with struct-level `#[serde(default)]` on package, if `[package]` table is entirely absent → Package::default() → name="" → validate: "invalid package name" + help. If `[package]` present but name missing → serde error "missing field `name` in `package`" — decent. Hmm, actually let me add a default (empty) for name too so both paths give the same friendly validation error:
    #[serde(default)]
    pub name: String,
    #[serde(default = "default_version")]
    pub version: String,
    #[serde(rename = "type", default = "default_type")]
    pub kind: String,
}
```
That's uniform: any partial manifest parses, and validate() reports what's missing.

```rust
    #[serde(default = "default_c_std")]

}
```
With `#[serde(default)]` on `pub build: Build` (so an absent `[build]` → Build::default()), plus field-level defaults for partial `[build]`.

Manifest methods:
```rust
impl Manifest {
    pub fn load(path: &Path) -> anyhow::Result<Self> {
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
            .with_context(|| format!("reading manifest {}", path.display()))?;
        let manifest: Manifest = toml::from_str(&text)
            .with_context(|| format!("parsing manifest {}", path.display()))?;
        manifest.validate()?;
        Ok(manifest)
    }

    pub fn save(&self, path: &Path) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        self.validate()?;
        let mut doc: toml_edit::DocumentMut = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {
            Ok(existing) if !existing.trim().is_empty() => existing
                .parse()
                .with_context(|| format!("parsing existing manifest {}", path.display()))?,
            _ => toml::to_string_pretty(self)
                .context("serializing manifest")?
                .parse()
                .context("serializing manifest")?,
        };
        apply_to_doc(self, &mut doc);
        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() { if !parent.as_os_str().is_empty() { std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)... } }
        std::fs::write(path, doc.to_string()).with_context(...)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn validate(&self) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
        validate_name(&self.package.name)?;
        if self.package.version.is_empty() {
            anyhow::bail!("package.version is empty\nhelp: set version to a semver string, e.g. version = \"0.1.0\"");
        }
        match self.package.kind.as_str() {
            "bin" | "lib" | "both" => {}
            other => anyhow::bail!("invalid package type {other:?}\nhelp: type must be one of: \"bin\", \"lib\", \"both\""),
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}
```

Hmm wait — a "lib"-only project: does the scaffold handle it? `new` has only `--lib` → "both". A lib-only type can only come from hand-editing the manifest (T006+). The scaffold: new always creates main.cpp (bin included in both "bin" and "both"). lib-only manifests aren't created by the T002 scaffold — fine. DESIGN's CMake says "add_executable(demo src/main.cpp) # only if bin" — that's for the T003 generator. In T002, new → bin or both; both include main.cpp.

Hmm — should cmd_new handle lib-only? No: `new <name>` → bin; `new <name> --lib` → both. Per task.

validate_name — free function in manifest.rs:
```rust
pub fn is_valid_name(name: &str) -> bool {
    let mut chars = name.chars();
    match chars.next() {
        Some(c) if c.is_ascii_lowercase() || c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '_' => {}
        _ => return false,
    }
    chars.all(|c| c.is_ascii_lowercase() || c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '_' || c == '-')
}

pub fn validate_name(name: &str) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    anyhow::ensure!(
        is_valid_name(name),
        "invalid project name {name:?}\nhelp: names must match [a-z0-9_][a-z0-9_-]* (lowercase letters, digits, '_', '-'; must not start with '-')",
    );
    Ok(())
}
```
Note: `anyhow::ensure!` with a format string — `ensure!(cond, "msg {x}")` works (it's a macro that formats).

conan_ref:
```rust
/// Maps a manifest dependency spec to a Conan reference (DESIGN.md §4).
pub fn conan_ref(pkg: &str, version: &str) -> String {
    let first = version.chars().next();
    match first {
        Some(c) if matches!(c, '>' | '<' | '=' | '^' | '~') => format!("{pkg}/[{version}]"),
        None => format!("{pkg}/*"),
        _ => format!("{pkg}/{version}"),
    }
}
```

apply_to_doc (for save):
```rust
fn apply_to_doc(m: &Manifest, doc: &mut toml_edit::DocumentMut) {
    doc["package"]["name"] = toml_edit::value(m.package.name.clone());
    doc["package"]["version"] = toml_edit::value(m.package.version.clone());
    doc["package"]["type"] = toml_edit::value(m.package.kind.clone());
    doc["build"]["cpp_std"] = toml_edit::value(m.build.cpp_std.clone());
    doc["build"]["c_std"] = toml_edit::value(m.build.c_std.clone());
    if doc.get("dependencies").and_then(Item::as_table_like).is_none() {
        doc["dependencies"] = toml_edit::Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
    }
    if let Some(deps) = doc["dependencies"].as_table_like_mut() {
        for (k, v) in &m.dependencies {
            deps.insert(k, toml_edit::value(v.clone()));
        }
        let stale: Vec<String> = deps.iter()
            .map(|(k, _)| k.to_string())
            .filter(|k| !m.dependencies.contains_key(k))
            .collect();
        for k in stale { deps.remove(&k); }
    }
}
```

And to ensure a table:
```rust
fn ensure_table<'a>(t: &'a mut toml_edit::Table, key: &str) -> &'a mut toml_edit::Table {
    match t.get(key) {
        Some(Item::Table(existing)) => {
            t.get_mut(key).unwrap().as_table_mut().unwrap()
        }
        _ => {
            t.insert(key, Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new()));
            t.get_mut(key).unwrap().as_table_mut().unwrap()
        }
    }
}
```
Borrow check: the first arm of the match immutably borrows t (t.get), then mutably borrows in the arm — NLL: the borrow from the scrutinee ends... in a `match t.get(key) { Some(Item::Table(..)) => t.get_mut(key)...}` — the pattern matches on `Option<&Item>`; the temporary borrow in the scrutinee lives through the match. `t.get_mut` inside the arm — conflict? The match scrutinee's borrow `t.get(key)` returns `Option<&Item>` — the reference is held during the arm execution. So `t.get_mut(key)` inside the arm = immutable borrow active + mutable borrow = error. Workaround:
```rust
match t.get(key).and_then(Item::as_table) {
    Some(_) => t.get_mut(key).unwrap().as_table_mut().unwrap(),
    None => { t.insert(key, ...); t.get_mut(key).unwrap().as_table_mut().unwrap() }
}
```
Still, `t.get(key).and_then(...)` evaluates to `Option<&Table>` — the borrow still lives during the arm. Ugh. Two steps:
```rust
let is_table = t.get(key).and_then(Item::as_table).is_some();
if !is_table { t.insert(key, Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new())); }
t.get_mut(key).expect("...").as_table_mut().expect("...")
```
Clean, no borrow trouble. (If the key exists as something else — say a value — we'd overwrite it with a table. Acceptable for save; malformed input.)

But wait — if Table doesn't have a public `insert`, all of this falls apart. Fallback if no `insert`: use `IndexMut` auto-insertion: `t[key] = Item::Table(Table::new());` — hmm, does Table's IndexMut auto-insert? For DocumentMut, the README documents auto-insertion via indexing. Table probably the same (same underlying impl).

The T002 spec: "save via toml_edit (parse existing doc, set values, write back) so that user comments/format survive when T006 edits deps. For now, save can start from the parsed existing file if present, else from toml::to_string_pretty."

"Can start from" — gives latitude. The minimal compliant version:
```rust
pub fn save(&self, path: &Path) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    self.validate()?;
    let _ = path; // ensure parent exists
    let text = match std::fs::read_to_string(path).ok().filter(|s| !s.trim().is_empty()) {
        Some(existing) => {
            // Start from the existing document and update values via toml_edit,
            // so user comments/format survive.
            let mut doc: toml_edit::DocumentMut = existing.parse()...;
            apply...
            doc.to_string()
        }
        None => toml::to_string_pretty(self)?,
    };
    std::fs::write(path, text)?;
    Ok(())
}
```

Scaffold functions:

```rust
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use anyhow::{bail, Context};

use crate::manifest::{self, Manifest};

fn render(template: &str, name: &str, pkg_type: &str, ident: &str) -> String {
    template.replace("{name}", name).replace("{type}", pkg_type).replace("{ident}", ident)
}

fn cpp_ident(name: &str) -> String {
    name.replace('-', "_")
}

/// Files to write for a scaffold: (relative path, content).
fn files_for(name: &str, pkg_type: &str) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    let ident = cpp_ident(name);
    let mut files: Vec<(String, String)> = Vec::new();
    let add = |files: &mut Vec<(String, String)>, path: &str, content: String| {
        files.push((path.to_string(), content));
    };
    add(&mut files, "CARRY.toml".into(), render(CARRY_TOML, name, pkg_type, &ident));
    add(&mut files, ".gitignore".into(), GITIGNORE.into());
    add(&mut files, "conanfile.txt".into(), CONANFILE_PLACEHOLDER.into());
    add(&mut files, "CMakeLists.txt".into(), render(CMAKELISTS_PLACEHOLDER, name, pkg_type, &ident));
    let main = if pkg_type == "both" { MAIN_CPP_BOTH } else { MAIN_CPP_BIN };
    add(&mut files, "src/main.cpp".into(), render(main, name, pkg_type, &ident));
    if pkg_type == "lib" || pkg_type == "both" {
        add(&mut files, format!("include/{name}/{name}.hpp"), render(LIB_HPP, name, pkg_type, &ident));
        add(&mut files, "src/lib.cpp".into(), render(LIB_CPP, name, pkg_type, &ident));
    }
    add(&mut files, "tests/test_main.cpp".into(), TEST_MAIN_CPP.into());
    files
}
```
Hmm — a closure `add` with &mut capture: fine, or just push directly. Let me drop the closure; just push.

```rust
pub fn cmd_new(name: &str, lib: bool) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    manifest::validate_name(name)?;
    let pkg_type = if lib { "both" } else { "bin" };
    let root = Path::new(name);
    match std::fs::metadata(root) {
        Ok(_) => {
            if root.join("CARRY.toml").is_file() {
                bail!("{name} is already a carry project\nhelp: cd into it and run 'carry build', or pick a different name");
            }
            bail!("directory {name} exists and is not a carry project\nhelp: remove it or pick a different name");
        }
        Err(err) if err.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {}
        Err(err) => return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("checking {name}")),
    }
    write_scaffold(root, name, pkg_type)
        .with_context(|| format!("creating project in {name}"))?;
    println!("created carry project {name} (type: {pkg_type})");
    print tree...
    println!();
    println!("next: cd {name} && carry build");
    Ok(())
}
```

```rust
fn write_scaffold(root: &Path, name: &str, pkg_type: &str) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let files = files_for(name, pkg_type);
    for (rel, content) in &files {
        let path = root.join(rel);
        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).with_context(|| format!("creating {}", parent.display()))?;
        }
        std::fs::write(path, content).with_context(|| format!("writing {}", path.display()))?;
    }
    // Print the created tree, one file per line
    for (rel, _) in &files {
        println!("  {rel}");
    }
    Ok(())
}
```
Print: "print the created tree (one file per line)". Print relative paths. Should I also print directories? "One file per line" — files only.

```rust
fn write_scaffold(root: &Path, name: &str, pkg_type: &str) -> anyhow::Result<Vec<String>> {
    let files = files_for(name, pkg_type);
    let mut rels = Vec::new();
    for (rel, content) in &files {
        let path = root.join(rel);
        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).with_context(|| format!("creating {}", parent.display()))?;
        }
        std::fs::write(path, content).with_context(|| format!("writing {}", path.display()))?;
        rels.push(rel.clone());
    }
    Ok(rels)
}
```

So:
```rust
fn run(cli: Cli) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let not_implemented = |cmd: &str, planned: &str| -> anyhow::Error {
        anyhow::anyhow!("{cmd} not implemented yet (planned in {planned})")
    };
    match cli.command {
        Commands::New { name, lib } => template::cmd_new(&name, lib),
        Commands::Init => template::cmd_init(),
        Commands::Build { .. } => Err(not_implemented("build", "T004")),
        Commands::Run { .. } => Err(not_implemented("run", "T005")),
        Commands::Test => Err(not_implemented("test", "T005")),
        Commands::Clean => Err(not_implemented("clean", "T005")),
        Commands::Add { .. } => Err(not_implemented("add", "T006")),
        Commands::Remove { .. } => Err(not_implemented("remove", "T006")),
    }
}
```
Wait — the match arms: `template::cmd_new(&name, lib)` returns Result — the match's type is Result. `Err(not_implemented(...))` is the same.

```rust
fn test_dir(name: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
    let dir = std::path::Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("target").join("tmp").join(name);
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
    dir
}
```

```rust
#[test]
fn save_preserves_existing_comments() {
    let dir = test_dir("manifest-comments");
    let path = dir.join("CARRY.toml");
    std::fs::write(&path, "# my project\n[package]\nname = \"demo\"\n# pinned version\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ntype = \"bin\"\n").unwrap();
    let mut m = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
    m.dependencies.insert("fmt".into(), "10.2.1".into());
    m.save(&path).unwrap();
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap();
    assert!(text.contains("# my project"));
    assert!(text.contains("# pinned version"));
    let m2 = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(m2.dependencies.get("fmt").map(String::as_str), Some("10.2.1"));
}
```

write_scaffold:
```rust
fn write_scaffold(root: &Path, name: &str, pkg_type: &str) -> anyhow::Result<Vec<String>> {
    let manifest = Manifest { ... };
    manifest.save(root.join("CARRY.toml"))
        .with_context(|| format!("writing {}", root.join("CARRY.toml").display()))?;
    let mut created = vec!["CARRY.toml".to_string()];
    for (rel, content) in files_for(name, pkg_type) {  // everything except CARRY.toml
        ...
        created.push(rel);
    }
    Ok(created)
}
```

```diff
//! CARRY.toml manifest: serde schema, load/save, validation, conan ref mapping.
//!
//! The manifest is the single source of truth for a carry project (DESIGN.md §4).
//! `save` goes through `toml_edit` so user comments and formatting survive edits
//! (the format-preserving `add`/`remove` commands build on this in T006).

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::Path;

use anyhow::{Context, Result};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use toml_edit::Item;

const VERSION_DEFAULT: &str = "0.1.0";
const TYPE_DEFAULT: &str = "bin";
const CPP_STD_DEFAULT: &str = "c++23";
const C_STD_DEFAULT: &str = "c11";

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Manifest {
    #[serde(default)]
    pub package: Package,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub build: Build,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub dependencies: BTreeMap<String, String>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Package {
    #[serde(default)]
    pub name: String,
    #[serde(default = "default_version")]
    pub version: String,
    #[serde(rename = "type", default = "default_type")]
    pub kind: String,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Build {
    #[serde(default = "default_cpp_std")]
    pub cpp_std: String,
    #[serde(default = "default_c_std")]
    pub c_std: String,
}

fn default_version() -> String {
    VERSION_DEFAULT.to_string()
}

fn default_type() -> String {
    TYPE_DEFAULT.to_string()
}

fn default_cpp_std() -> String {
    CPP_STD_DEFAULT.to_string()
}

fn default_c_std() -> String {
    C_STD_DEFAULT.to_string()
}

impl Default for Package {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            name: String::new(),
            version: default_version(),
            kind: default_type(),
        }
    }
}

impl Default for Build {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            cpp_std: default_cpp_std(),
            c_std: default_c_std(),
        }
    }
}

impl Manifest {
    /// Read and parse `CARRY.toml` from `path`, then validate it.
    pub fn load(path: &Path) -> Result<Self> {
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
            .with_context(|| format!("reading {}", path.display()))?;
        let manifest: Self = toml::from_str(&text)
            .with_context(|| format!("parsing {}", path.display()))?;
        manifest.validate()?;
        Ok(manifest)
    }

    /// Write the manifest to `path` via `toml_edit`: an existing file is parsed
    /// first and updated in place (comments/formatting survive); a missing or
    /// empty file is written from scratch.
    pub fn save(&self, path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
        self.validate()?;
        let existing = std::fs::read_to_string(path).ok();
        let mut doc: toml_edit::DocumentMut = match existing.as_deref() {
            Some(text) if !text.trim().is_empty() => text
                .parse()
                .with_context(|| format!("parsing existing {}", path.display()))?,
            _ => toml::to_string_pretty(self)
                .context("serializing manifest")?
                .parse()
                .context("serializing manifest")?,
        };
        apply_to_doc(self, &mut doc);
        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
            if !parent.as_os_str().is_empty() {
                std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
                    .with_context(|| format!("creating {}", parent.display()))?;
            }
        }
        std::fs::write(path, doc.to_string())
            .with_context(|| format!("writing {}", path.display()))?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Check schema rules; returns errors with `help:` hints (DESIGN.md §8).
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<()> {
        validate_name(&self.package.name)?;
        if self.package.version.is_empty() {
            anyhow::bail!(
                "package.version is empty\n\
                 help: set a semver string, e.g. version = \"0.1.0\""
            );
        }
        match self.package.kind.as_str() {
            "bin" | "lib" | "both" => {}
            other => anyhow::bail!(
                "invalid package type {other:?}\n\
                 help: type must be one of: \"bin\", \"lib\", \"both\""
            ),
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// Set every manifest value into an (existing or fresh) toml_edit document.
fn apply_to_doc(m: &Manifest, doc: &mut toml_edit::DocumentMut) {
    let pkg = ensure_table(doc, "package");
    pkg["name"] = Item::Value(toml_edit::value(m.package.name.clone()));
    pkg["version"] = Item::Value(toml_edit::value(m.package.version.clone()));
    pkg["type"] = Item::Value(toml_edit::value(m.package.kind.clone()));
    let build = ensure_table(doc, "build");
    build["cpp_std"] = Item::Value(toml_edit::value(m.build.cpp_std.clone()));
    build["c_std"] = Item::Value(toml_edit::value(m.build.c_std.clone()));
    let deps = ensure_table(doc, "dependencies");
    for (name, version) in &m.dependencies {
        deps[name.as_str()] = Item::Value(toml_edit::value(version.clone()));
    }
    let stale: Vec<String> = deps
        .iter()
        .map(|(name, _)| name.to_string())
        .filter(|name| !m.dependencies.contains_key(name))
        .collect();
    for name in stale {
        deps.remove(&name);
    }
}

/// Return the named root table of `doc`, creating it if missing or non-table.
fn ensure_table<'a>(doc: &'a mut toml_edit::DocumentMut, key: &str) -> &'a mut toml_edit::Table {
    let is_table = doc.get(key).and_then(Item::as_table).is_some();
    if !is_table {
        doc.insert(key, Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new()));
    }
    doc.get_mut(key)
        .and_then(Item::as_table_mut)
        .expect("table (re)created above")
}

/// A valid project name: `[a-z0-9_][a-z0-9_-]*` (DESIGN.md §4).
pub fn is_valid_name(name: &str) -> bool {
    let mut chars = name.chars();
    let first = matches!(chars.next(), Some(c) if c.is_ascii_lowercase() || c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '_');
    first && chars.all(|c| c.is_ascii_lowercase() || c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '_' || c == '-')
}

/// Validate a project name, with a `help:` hint on failure.
pub fn validate_name(name: &str) -> Result<()> {
    anyhow::ensure!(
        is_valid_name(name),
        "invalid project name {name:?}\n\
         help: names must match [a-z0-9_][a-z0-9_-] \
         (lowercase letters, digits, '_', '-'; must not start with '-')"
    );
    Ok(())
}

/// Map a manifest dependency value to a Conan reference (DESIGN.md §4):
/// plain version → `pkg/ver`, range (`>` `<` `=` `^` `~` prefix) → `pkg/[range]`,
/// empty → `pkg/*`.
pub fn conan_ref(pkg: &str, version: &str) -> String {
    match version.chars().next() {
        Some(c) if matches!(c, '>' | '<' | '=' | '^' | '~') => format!("{pkg}/[{version}]"),
        None => format!("{pkg}/*"),
        _ => format!("{pkg}/{version}"),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const FULL: &str = "\
[package]
name = \"demo\"
version = \"0.1.0\"
type = \"both\"

[build]
cpp_std = \"c++23\"
c_std = \"c11\"

[dependencies]
fmt = \"10.2.1\"
zeta = \">=1.0\"
";

    /// Deterministic scratch dir under target/ (gitignored) for file-based tests.
    fn test_dir(name: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
        let dir = Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
            .join("target")
            .join("tmp")
            .join(name);
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
        dir
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_full_manifest() {
        let m: Manifest = toml::from_str(FULL).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(m.package.name, "demo");
        assert_eq!(m.package.version, "0.1.0");
        assert_eq!(m.package.kind, "both");
        assert_eq!(m.build.cpp_std, "c++23");
        assert_eq!(m.build.c_std, "c11");
        assert_eq!(m.dependencies.get("fmt").map(String::as_str), Some("10.2.1"));
        assert_eq!(m.dependencies.get("zeta").map(String::as_str), Some(">=1.0"));
        assert_eq!(m.dependencies.len(), 2);
        m.validate().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_minimal_manifest_with_defaults() {
        let m: Manifest = toml::from_str("[package]\nname = \"demo\"\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(m.package.version, "0.1.0");
        assert_eq!(m.package.kind, "bin");
        assert_eq!(m.build.cpp_std, "c++23");
        assert_eq!(m.build.c_std, "c11");
        assert!(m.dependencies.is_empty());
        m.validate().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn save_load_roundtrip() {
        let dir = test_dir("manifest-roundtrip");
        let path = dir.join("CARRY.toml");
        let m: Manifest = toml::from_str(FULL).unwrap();
        m.save(&path).unwrap();
        let m2 = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(m, m2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn save_preserves_existing_comments() {
        let dir = test_dir("manifest-comments");
        let path = dir.join("CARRY.toml");
        std::fs::write(
            &path,
            "# my project\n\
             [package]\n\
             name = \"demo\"\n\
             # pinned version\n\
             version = \"0.1.0\"\n\
             type = \"bin\"\n",
        )
        .unwrap();
        let mut m = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        m.dependencies.insert("fmt".to_string(), "10.2.1".to_string());
        m.save(&path).unwrap();
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap();
        assert!(text.contains("# my project"), "header comment lost:\n{text}");
        assert!(text.contains("# pinned version"), "inline comment lost:\n{text}");
        let m2 = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(m2.dependencies.get("fmt").map(String::as_str), Some("10.2.1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn save_removes_stale_dependencies() {
        let dir = test_dir("manifest-stale-deps");
        let path = dir.join("CARRY.toml");
        std::fs::write(
            &path,
            "[package]\nname = \"demo\"\n\n[dependencies]\nold = \"1.0\"\n",
        )
        .unwrap();
        let mut m = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        m.dependencies.clear();
        m.dependencies.insert("fmt".to_string(), "10.2.1".to_string());
        m.save(&path).unwrap();
        let m2 = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        assert!(!m2.dependencies.contains_key("old"));
        assert_eq!(m2.dependencies.get("fmt").map(String::as_str), Some("10.2.1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn name_validation_rules() {
        for ok in ["demo", "a", "foo-bar", "_x", "9lives", "a_b-c"] {
            assert!(is_valid_name(ok), "{ok:?} should be valid");
        }
        for bad in ["", "-leading", "Bad", "bad name", "bad/name", "böhm", "a?"] {
            assert!(!is_valid_name(bad), "{bad:?} should be invalid");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn validate_rejects_bad_name_type_version() {
        for text in [
            "[package]\nname = \"\"\n",
            "[package]\nname = \"Bad-Name\"\n",
            "[package]\nname = \"demo\"\nversion = \"\"\n",
            "[package]\nname = \"demo\"\ntype = \"exe\"\n",
        ] {
            let m: Manifest = toml::from_str(text).unwrap();
            let err = m.validate().unwrap_err().to_string();
            assert!(err.contains("help:"), "missing help hint in: {err}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn conan_ref_branches() {
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("fmt", "10.2.1"), "fmt/10.2.1");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("fmt", ">=10"), "fmt/[>=10]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("z", "<=2"), "z/[<=2]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("z", "=1.2.3"), "z/[=1.2.3]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("z", "^1"), "z/[^1]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("z", "~1.2"), "z/[~1.2]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("fmt", ""), "fmt/*");
    }
}
```

```diff
//! Scaffold contents and file writers for `carry new` / `carry init`.
//!
//! Templates are `&'static str` constants; `{name}`, `{type}` and `{ident}` are
//! substituted with plain `str::replace` (no `format!` on user input).
//!
//! `{ident}` is the C++ identifier form of the project name: the name rule
//! allows `-`, which is not a valid C++ identifier character, so dashes become
//! underscores in namespaces/identifiers (file paths keep the raw name).

use std::path::Path;

use anyhow::{bail, Context, Result};

use crate::manifest::{self, Build, Manifest, Package};

/// Placeholder conanfile.txt — T003 replaces it with the real render.
pub const CONANFILE_PLACEHOLDER: &str = "\
[generators]
CMakeDeps
CMakeToolchain
";

/// Minimal valid CMakeLists.txt stub — T003 replaces it with the real render.
pub const CMAKELISTS_PLACEHOLDER: &str = "\
# generated by carry — replaced by real render in T003
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.21)
project({name} VERSION 0.1.0 LANGUAGES C CXX)
";

pub const GITIGNORE: &str = "build/\n";

/// main.cpp for type "bin" (no library to call).
pub const MAIN_CPP_BIN: &str = "\
#include <iostream>

int main() {
  std::cout << \"Hello from {name}!\" << std::endl;
  return 0;
}
";

/// main.cpp for type "both" (calls the library's greeting).
pub const MAIN_CPP_BOTH: &str = "\
#include <iostream>

#include \"{name}/{name}.hpp\"

int main() {
  std::cout << {ident}::greeting() << std::endl;
  return 0;
}
";

/// include/<name>/<name>.hpp
pub const LIB_HPP: &str = "\
#pragma once

#include <string>

namespace {ident} {

std::string greeting();

}  // namespace {ident}
";

/// src/lib.cpp
pub const LIB_CPP: &str = "\
#include \"{name}/{name}.hpp\"

namespace {ident} {

std::string greeting() {
  return \"Hello from {name}!\";
}

}  // namespace {ident}
";

/// tests/test_main.cpp — trivial assert-based test binary, no framework.
pub const TEST_MAIN_CPP: &str = "\
#include <cassert>
#include <cstdio>

int main() {
  assert(1 + 1 == 2);  // placeholder — real project tests go here
  std::puts(\"all tests passed\");
  return 0;
}
";

fn render(template: &str, name: &str, pkg_type: &str, ident: &str) -> String {
    template
        .replace("{name}", name)
        .replace("{type}", pkg_type)
        .replace("{ident}", ident)
}

/// C++ identifier form of the project name (dashes → underscores).
pub fn cpp_ident(name: &str) -> String {
    name.replace('-', "_")
}

/// All scaffold files except CARRY.toml (written separately via `Manifest::save`):
/// (relative path, rendered content), in creation order.
fn files_for(name: &str, pkg_type: &str) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    let ident = cpp_ident(name);
    let mut files: Vec<(String, String)> = vec![
        (".gitignore".to_string(), GITIGNORE.to_string()),
        ("conanfile.txt".to_string(), CONANFILE_PLACEHOLDER.to_string()),
        (
            "CMakeLists.txt".to_string(),
            render(CMAKELISTS_PLACEHOLDER, name, pkg_type, &ident),
        ),
    ];
    let main = if pkg_type == "both" { MAIN_CPP_BOTH } else { MAIN_CPP_BIN };
    files.push((
        "src/main.cpp".to_string(),
        render(main, name, pkg_type, &ident),
    ));
    if pkg_type == "lib" || pkg_type == "both" {
        files.push((
            format!("include/{name}/{name}.hpp"),
            render(LIB_HPP, name, pkg_type, &ident),
        ));
        files.push((
            "src/lib.cpp".to_string(),
            render(LIB_CPP, name, pkg_type, &ident),
        ));
    }
    files.push(("tests/test_main.cpp".to_string(), TEST_MAIN_CPP.to_string()));
    files
}

/// Create the scaffold under `root`. Returns the created paths, relative to
/// `root`, in creation order.
fn write_scaffold(root: &Path, name: &str, pkg_type: &str) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
    let manifest = Manifest {
        package: Package {
            name: name.to_string(),
            version: "0.1.0".to_string(),
            kind: pkg_type.to_string(),
        },
        build: Build::default(),
        dependencies: std::collections::BTreeMap::new(),
    };
    let mut created = Vec::new();
    let carry_toml = root.join("CARRY.toml");
    manifest.save(&carry_toml).with_context(|| {
        format!(
            "writing {}",
            carry_toml.strip_prefix(root).unwrap_or(&carry_toml).display()
        )
    })?;
    created.push("CARRY.toml".to_string());
    for (rel, content) in files_for(name, pkg_type) {
        let path = root.join(&rel);
        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
                .with_context(|| format!("creating {}", parent.display()))?;
        }
        std::fs::write(&path, content)
            .with_context(|| format!("writing {}", path.display()))?;
        created.push(rel);
    }
    Ok(created)
}

fn print_created(created: &[String]) {
    for rel in created {
        println!("  {rel}");
    }
}

/// `carry new <name> [--lib]` — create `./<name>/` with the full scaffold.
pub fn cmd_new(name: &str, lib: bool) -> Result<()> {
    manifest::validate_name(name)?;
    let pkg_type = if lib { "both" } else { "bin" };
    let root = Path::new(name);
    match std::fs::metadata(root) {
        Ok(meta) => {
            if !meta.is_dir() {
                bail!("{name} exists and is not a directory");
            }
            if root.join("CARRY.toml").is_file() {
                bail!("{name} is already a carry project\nhelp: cd into it and run 'carry build'");
            }
            let has_entries = std::fs::read_dir(root)
                .with_context(|| format!("reading {name}"))?
                .any(std::result::Result::is_ok);
            if has_entries {
                bail!("{name} dir exists and is not a carry project\nhelp: remove it or pick a different name");
            }
        }
        Err(err) if err.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {}
        Err(err) => return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("checking {name}")),
    }
    let created = write_scaffold(root, name, pkg_type)
        .with_context(|| format!("creating project in {name}"))?;
    println!("created carry project {name} (type: {pkg_type})");
    print_created(&created);
    println!();
    println!("next: cd {name} && carry build");
    Ok(())
}

/// `carry init` — turn the current directory into a carry project (type "bin",
/// name from the directory name).
pub fn cmd_init() -> Result<()> {
    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().context("reading current directory")?;
    let name = cwd
        .file_name()
        .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
        .unwrap_or("")
        .to_string();
    manifest::validate_name(&name)?;
    if cwd.join("CARRY.toml").is_file() {
        bail!("already a carry project\nhelp: run 'carry build' instead");
    }
    let created = write_scaffold(&cwd, &name, "bin")
        .with_context(|| format!("initializing carry project in {}", cwd.display()))?;
    println!("initialized carry project {name} (type: bin)");
    print_created(&created);
    println!();
    println!("next: carry build");
    Ok(())
}

```

```diff
//! carry — a C/C++ project manager in the spirit of Cargo.
//!
//! T001: CLI scaffold. All MVP subcommands from DESIGN.md §2 are defined.
//! T002: `new`/`init` implemented (manifest + scaffold); the rest are stubs
//! returning "not implemented" until their owning task lands
//! (build → T004, run/test/clean → T005, add/remove → T006).

// Module files are empty scaffolds until their owning tasks fill them in;
// remove the allow on each as it becomes used (T004–T006).
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod build;
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod generate;
mod manifest;
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod project;
mod template;

use clap::{Parser, Subcommand};

#[derive(Parser)]
#[command(name = "carry", version, about = "A C/C++ project manager in the spirit of Cargo")]
struct Cli {
    /// Print each external command before running it (wired up in T004)
    #[arg(long, global = true)]
    #[allow(dead_code)]
    verbose: bool,

    #[command(subcommand)]
    command: Commands,
}

#[derive(Subcommand)]
enum Commands {
    /// Create a new carry project at ./<name>/
    New {
        /// Project name
        name: String,

        /// Also add a library target
        #[arg(long)]
        lib: bool,
    },

    /// Turn the current directory into a carry project
    Init,

    /// Regenerate files, install dependencies, configure, and build
    Build {
        /// Build with the Release build type (default: Debug)
        #[arg(long)]
        release: bool,
    },

    /// Build (Debug) then run the project binary with the given arguments
    Run {
        #[arg(trailing_var_arg = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
        args: Vec<String>,
    },

    /// Build (Debug) then run the test suite
    Test,

    /// Remove the build/ directory
    Clean,

    /// Add or update a dependency (e.g. `fmt` or `fmt/10.2.1`)
    Add {
        /// Package spec
        pkg: String,
    },

    /// Remove a dependency
    Remove {
        /// Package name
        pkg: String,
    },
}

fn main() {
    let cli = Cli::parse();
    let not_implemented = |cmd: &str, planned: &str| -> ! {
        eprintln!("error: {cmd} not implemented yet (planned in {planned})");
        std::process::exit(1);
    };
    match cli.command {
        Commands::New { .. } => not_implemented("new", "T002"),
        Commands::Init => not_implemented("init", "T002"),
        Commands::Build { .. } => not_implemented("build", "T004"),
        Commands::Run { .. } => not_implemented("run", "T005"),
        Commands::Test => not_implemented("test", "T005"),
        Commands::Clean => not_implemented("clean", "T005"),
        Commands::Add { .. } => not_implemented("add", "T006"),
        Commands::Remove { .. } => not_implemented("remove", "T006"),
    }
}
```

```diff
//! carry — a C/C++ project manager in the spirit of Cargo.
//!
//! T001: CLI scaffold. All MVP subcommands from DESIGN.md §2 are defined.
//! T002: `new`/`init` implemented (manifest + scaffold); the rest are stubs
//! returning "not implemented" until their owning task lands
//! (build → T004, run/test/clean → T005, add/remove → T006).

// Module files are empty scaffolds until their owning tasks fill them in;
// remove the allow on each as it becomes used (T004–T006).
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod build;
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod generate;
mod manifest;
#[allow(dead_code)]
mod project;
mod template;

use clap::{Parser, Subcommand};

#[derive(Parser)]
#[command(name = "carry", version, about = "A C/C++ project manager in the spirit of Cargo")]
struct Cli {
    /// Print each external command before running it (wired up in T004)
    #[arg(long, global = true)]
    #[allow(dead_code)]
    verbose: bool,

    #[command(subcommand)]
    command: Commands,
}

#[derive(Subcommand)]
enum Commands {
    /// Create a new carry project at ./<name>/
    New {
        /// Project name
        name: String,

        /// Also add a library target
        #[arg(long)]
        lib: bool,
    },

    /// Turn the current directory into a carry project
    Init,

    /// Regenerate files, install dependencies, configure, and build
    Build {
        /// Build with the Release build type (default: Debug)
        #[arg(long)]
        release: bool,
    },

    /// Build (Debug) then run the project binary with the given arguments
    Run {
        #[arg(trailing_var_arg = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
        args: Vec<String>,
    },

    /// Build (Debug) then run the test suite
    Test,

    /// Remove the build/ directory
    Clean,

    /// Add or update a dependency (e.g. `fmt` or `fmt/10.2.1`)
    Add {
        /// Package spec
        pkg: String,
    },

    /// Remove a dependency
    Remove {
        /// Package name
        pkg: String,
    },
}

fn main() {
    let cli = Cli::parse();
    if let Err(err) = run(cli) {
        eprintln!("error: {err}");
        std::process::exit(1);
    }
}

fn run(cli: Cli) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let not_implemented = |cmd: &str, planned: &str| -> anyhow::Error {
        anyhow::anyhow!("{cmd} not implemented yet (planned in {planned})")
    };
    match cli.command {
        Commands::New { name, lib } => template::cmd_new(&name, lib),
        Commands::Init => template::cmd_init(),
        Commands::Build { .. } => Err(not_implemented("build", "T004")),
        Commands::Run { .. } => Err(not_implemented("run", "T005")),
        Commands::Test => Err(not_implemented("test", "T005")),
        Commands::Clean => Err(not_implemented("clean", "T005")),
        Commands::Add { .. } => Err(not_implemented("add", "T006")),
        Commands::Remove { .. } => Err(not_implemented("remove", "T006")),
    }
}

```

```json
}
```

And `ensure_table`:
```rust
fn ensure_table<'a>(doc: &'a mut toml_edit::DocumentMut, key: &str) -> &'a mut toml_edit::Table {
    let is_table = doc.get(key).and_then(Item::as_table).is_some();
    if !is_table {
        doc.insert(key, toml_edit::Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new()));
    }
    doc.get_mut(key).and_then(Item::as_table_mut).expect("table (re)created above")
}
```

```diff
//! CARRY.toml manifest: serde schema, load/save, validation, conan ref mapping.
//!
//! The manifest is the single source of truth for a carry project (DESIGN.md §4).
//! `save` goes through `toml_edit` so user comments and formatting survive edits
//! (the format-preserving `add`/`remove` commands build on this in T006).

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::Path;

use anyhow::{Context, Result};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use toml_edit::Item;

const VERSION_DEFAULT: &str = "0.1.0";
const TYPE_DEFAULT: &str = "bin";
const CPP_STD_DEFAULT: &str = "c++23";
const C_STD_DEFAULT: &str = "c11";

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Manifest {
    #[serde(default)]
    pub package: Package,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub build: Build,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub dependencies: BTreeMap<String, String>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Package {
    #[serde(default)]
    pub name: String,
    #[serde(default = "default_version")]
    pub version: String,
    #[serde(rename = "type", default = "default_type")]
    pub kind: String,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Build {
    #[serde(default = "default_cpp_std")]
    pub cpp_std: String,
    #[serde(default = "default_c_std")]
    pub c_std: String,
}

fn default_version() -> String {
    VERSION_DEFAULT.to_string()
}

fn default_type() -> String {
    TYPE_DEFAULT.to_string()
}

fn default_cpp_std() -> String {
    CPP_STD_DEFAULT.to_string()
}

fn default_c_std() -> String {
    C_STD_DEFAULT.to_string()
}

impl Default for Package {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            name: String::new(),
            version: default_version(),
            kind: default_type(),
        }
    }
}

impl Default for Build {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            cpp_std: default_cpp_std(),
            c_std: default_c_std(),
        }
    }
}

impl Manifest {
    /// Read and parse `CARRY.toml` from `path`, then validate it.
    pub fn load(path: &Path) -> Result<Self> {
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
            .with_context(|| format!("reading {}", path.display()))?;
        let manifest: Self = toml::from_str(&text)
            .with_context(|| format!("parsing {}", path.display()))?;
        manifest.validate()?;
        Ok(manifest)
    }

    /// Write the manifest to `path` via `toml_edit`: an existing file is parsed
    /// first and updated in place (comments/formatting survive); a missing or
    /// empty file is written from scratch.
    pub fn save(&self, path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
        self.validate()?;
        let existing = std::fs::read_to_string(path).ok();
        let mut doc: toml_edit::DocumentMut = match existing.as_deref() {
            Some(text) if !text.trim().is_empty() => text
                .parse()
                .with_context(|| format!("parsing existing {}", path.display()))?,
            _ => toml::to_string_pretty(self)
                .context("serializing manifest")?
                .parse()
                .context("serializing manifest")?,
        };
        apply_to_doc(self, &mut doc);
        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
            if !parent.as_os_str().is_empty() {
                std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
                    .with_context(|| format!("creating {}", parent.display()))?;
            }
        }
        std::fs::write(path, doc.to_string())
            .with_context(|| format!("writing {}", path.display()))?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Check schema rules; returns errors with `help:` hints (DESIGN.md §8).
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<()> {
        validate_name(&self.package.name)?;
        if self.package.version.is_empty() {
            anyhow::bail!(
                "package.version is empty\n\
                 help: set a semver string, e.g. version = \"0.1.0\""
            );
        }
        match self.package.kind.as_str() {
            "bin" | "lib" | "both" => {}
            other => anyhow::bail!(
                "invalid package type {other:?}\n\
                 help: type must be one of: \"bin\", \"lib\", \"both\""
            ),
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// Set every manifest value into an (existing or fresh) toml_edit document.
fn apply_to_doc(m: &Manifest, doc: &mut toml_edit::DocumentMut) {
    let pkg = ensure_table(doc, "package");
    pkg.insert("name", toml_edit::value(m.package.name.clone()));
    pkg.insert("version", toml_edit::value(m.package.version.clone()));
    pkg.insert("type", toml_edit::value(m.package.kind.clone()));
    let build = ensure_table(doc, "build");
    build.insert("cpp_std", toml_edit::value(m.build.cpp_std.clone()));
    build.insert("c_std", toml_edit::value(m.build.c_std.clone()));
    let deps = ensure_table(doc, "dependencies");
    for (name, version) in &m.dependencies {
        deps.insert(name, toml_edit::value(version.clone()));
    }
    let stale: Vec<String> = deps
        .iter()
        .map(|(name, _)| name.to_string())
        .filter(|name| !m.dependencies.contains_key(name))
        .collect();
    for name in stale {
        deps.remove(&name);
    }
}

/// Return the named root table of `doc`, creating it if missing or non-table.
fn ensure_table<'a>(doc: &'a mut toml_edit::DocumentMut, key: &str) -> &'a mut toml_edit::Table {
    let is_table = doc.get(key).and_then(Item::as_table).is_some();
    if !is_table {
        doc.insert(key, Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new()));
    }
    doc.get_mut(key)
        .and_then(Item::as_table_mut)
        .expect("table (re)created above")
}

/// A valid project name: `[a-z0-9_][a-z0-9_-]*` (DESIGN.md §4).
pub fn is_valid_name(name: &str) -> bool {
    let mut chars = name.chars();
    let first = matches!(chars.next(), Some(c) if c.is_ascii_lowercase() || c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '_');
    first && chars.all(|c| c.is_ascii_lowercase() || c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '_' || c == '-')
}

/// Validate a project name, with a `help:` hint on failure.
pub fn validate_name(name: &str) -> Result<()> {
    anyhow::ensure!(
        is_valid_name(name),
        "invalid project name {name:?}\n\
         help: names must match [a-z0-9_][a-z0-9_-] \
         (lowercase letters, digits, '_', '-'; must not start with '-')"
    );
    Ok(())
}

/// Map a manifest dependency value to a Conan reference (DESIGN.md §4):
/// plain version → `pkg/ver`, range (`>` `<` `=` `^` `~` prefix) → `pkg/[range]`,
/// empty → `pkg/*`.
pub fn conan_ref(pkg: &str, version: &str) -> String {
    match version.chars().next() {
        Some(c) if matches!(c, '>' | '<' | '=' | '^' | '~') => format!("{pkg}/[{version}]"),
        None => format!("{pkg}/*"),
        _ => format!("{pkg}/{version}"),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const FULL: &str = "\
[package]
name = \"demo\"
version = \"0.1.0\"
type = \"both\"

[build]
cpp_std = \"c++23\"
c_std = \"c11\"

[dependencies]
fmt = \"10.2.1\"
zeta = \">=1.0\"
";

    /// Deterministic scratch dir under target/ (gitignored) for file-based tests.
    fn test_dir(name: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
        let dir = Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
            .join("target")
            .join("tmp")
            .join(name);
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
        dir
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_full_manifest() {
        let m: Manifest = toml::from_str(FULL).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(m.package.name, "demo");
        assert_eq!(m.package.version, "0.1.0");
        assert_eq!(m.package.kind, "both");
        assert_eq!(m.build.cpp_std, "c++23");
        assert_eq!(m.build.c_std, "c11");
        assert_eq!(m.dependencies.get("fmt").map(String::as_str), Some("10.2.1"));
        assert_eq!(m.dependencies.get("zeta").map(String::as_str), Some(">=1.0"));
        assert_eq!(m.dependencies.len(), 2);
        m.validate().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_minimal_manifest_with_defaults() {
        let m: Manifest = toml::from_str("[package]\nname = \"demo\"\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(m.package.version, "0.1.0");
        assert_eq!(m.package.kind, "bin");
        assert_eq!(m.build.cpp_std, "c++23");
        assert_eq!(m.build.c_std, "c11");
        assert!(m.dependencies.is_empty());
        m.validate().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn save_load_roundtrip() {
        let dir = test_dir("manifest-roundtrip");
        let path = dir.join("CARRY.toml");
        let m: Manifest = toml::from_str(FULL).unwrap();
        m.save(&path).unwrap();
        let m2 = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(m, m2);
    }

    #[test]
    fn save_preserves_existing_comments() {
        let dir = test_dir("manifest-comments");
        let path = dir.join("CARRY.toml");
        std::fs::write(
            &path,
            "# my project\n\
             [package]\n\
             name = \"demo\"\n\
             # pinned version\n\
             version = \"0.1.0\"\n\
             type = \"bin\"\n",
        )
        .unwrap();
        let mut m = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        m.dependencies.insert("fmt".to_string(), "10.2.1".to_string());
        m.save(&path).unwrap();
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap();
        assert!(text.contains("# my project"), "header comment lost:\n{text}");
        assert!(text.contains("# pinned version"), "inline comment lost:\n{text}");
        let m2 = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(m2.dependencies.get("fmt").map(String::as_str), Some("10.2.1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn save_removes_stale_dependencies() {
        let dir = test_dir("manifest-stale-deps");
        let path = dir.join("CARRY.toml");
        std::fs::write(
            &path,
            "[package]\nname = \"demo\"\n\n[dependencies]\nold = \"1.0\"\n",
        )
        .unwrap();
        let mut m = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        m.dependencies.clear();
        m.dependencies.insert("fmt".to_string(), "10.2.1".to_string());
        m.save(&path).unwrap();
        let m2 = Manifest::load(&path).unwrap();
        assert!(!m2.dependencies.contains_key("old"));
        assert_eq!(m2.dependencies.get("fmt").map(String::as_str), Some("10.2.1"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn name_validation_rules() {
        for ok in ["demo", "a", "foo-bar", "_x", "9lives", "a_b-c"] {
            assert!(is_valid_name(ok), "{ok:?} should be valid");
        }
        for bad in ["", "-leading", "Bad", "bad name", "bad/name", "böhm", "a?"] {
            assert!(!is_valid_name(bad), "{bad:?} should be invalid");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn validate_rejects_bad_name_type_version() {
        for text in [
            "[package]\nname = \"\"\n",
            "[package]\nname = \"Bad-Name\"\n",
            "[package]\nname = \"demo\"\nversion = \"\"\n",
            "[package]\nname = \"demo\"\ntype = \"exe\"\n",
        ] {
            let m: Manifest = toml::from_str(text).unwrap();
            let err = m.validate().unwrap_err().to_string();
            assert!(err.contains("help:"), "missing help hint in: {err}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn conan_ref_branches() {
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("fmt", "10.2.1"), "fmt/10.2.1");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("fmt", ">=10"), "fmt/[>=10]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("z", "<=2"), "z/[<=2]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("z", "=1.2.3"), "z/[=1.2.3]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("z", "^1"), "z/[^1]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("z", "~1.2"), "z/[~1.2]");
        assert_eq!(conan_ref("fmt", ""), "fmt/*");
    }
}
```

```diff
//! Scaffold contents and file writers for `carry new` / `carry init`.
//!
//! Templates are `&'static str` constants; `{name}`, `{type}` and `{ident}` are
//! substituted with plain `str::replace` (no `format!` on user input).
//!
//! `{ident}` is the C++ identifier form of the project name: the name rule
//! allows `-`, which is not a valid C++ identifier character, so dashes become
//! underscores in namespaces/identifiers (file paths keep the raw name).

use std::path::Path;

use anyhow::{bail, Context, Result};

use crate::manifest::{self, Build, Manifest, Package};

/// Placeholder conanfile.txt — T003 replaces it with the real render.
pub const CONANFILE_PLACEHOLDER: &str = "\
[generators]
CMakeDeps
CMakeToolchain
";

/// Minimal valid CMakeLists.txt stub — T003 replaces it with the real render.
pub const CMAKELISTS_PLACEHOLDER: &str = "\
# generated by carry — replaced by real render in T003
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.21)
project({name} VERSION 0.1.0 LANGUAGES C CXX)
";

pub const GITIGNORE: &str = "build/\n";

/// main.cpp for type "bin" (no library to call).
pub const MAIN_CPP_BIN: &str = "\
#include <iostream>

int main() {
  std::cout << \"Hello from {name}!\" << std::endl;
  return 0;
}
";

/// main.cpp for type "both" (calls the library's greeting).
pub const MAIN_CPP_BOTH: &str = "\
#include <iostream>

#include \"{name}/{name}.hpp\"

int main() {
  std::cout << {ident}::greeting() << std::endl;
  return 0;
}
";

/// include/<name>/<name>.hpp
pub const LIB_HPP: &str = "\
#pragma once

#include <string>

namespace {ident} {

std::string greeting();

}  // namespace {ident}
";

/// src/lib.cpp
pub const LIB_CPP: &str = "\
#include \"{name}/{name}.hpp\"

namespace {ident} {

std::string greeting() {
  return \"Hello from {name}!\";
}

}  // namespace {ident}
";

/// tests/test_main.cpp — trivial assert-based test binary, no framework.
pub const TEST_MAIN_CPP: &str = "\
#include <cassert>
#include <cstdio>

int main() {
  assert(1 + 1 == 2);  // placeholder — real project tests go here
  std::puts(\"all tests passed\");
  return 0;
}
";

fn render(template: &str, name: &str, pkg_type: &str, ident: &str) -> String {
    template
        .replace("{name}", name)
        .replace("{type}", pkg_type)
        .replace("{ident}", ident)
}

/// C++ identifier form of the project name (dashes → underscores).
pub fn cpp_ident(name: &str) -> String {
    name.replace('-', "_")
}

/// All scaffold files except CARRY.toml (written separately via `Manifest::save`):
/// (relative path, rendered content), in creation order.
fn files_for(name: &str, pkg_type: &str) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    let ident = cpp_ident(name);
    let mut files: Vec<(String, String)> = vec![
        (".gitignore".to_string(), GITIGNORE.to_string()),
        ("conanfile.txt".to_string(), CONANFILE_PLACEHOLDER.to_string()),
        (
            "CMakeLists.txt".to_string(),
            render(CMAKELISTS_PLACEHOLDER, name, pkg_type, &ident),
        ),
    ];
    let main = if pkg_type == "both" { MAIN_CPP_BOTH } else { MAIN_CPP_BIN };
    files.push((
        "src/main.cpp".to_string(),
        render(main, name, pkg_type, &ident),
    ));
    if pkg_type == "lib" || pkg_type == "both" {
        files.push((
            format!("include/{name}/{name}.hpp"),
            render(LIB_HPP, name, pkg_type, &ident),
        ));
        files.push((
            "src/lib.cpp".to_string(),
            render(LIB_CPP, name, pkg_type, &ident),
        ));
    }
    files.push(("tests/test_main.cpp".to_string(), TEST_MAIN_CPP.to_string()));
    files
}

/// Create the scaffold under `root`. Returns the created paths, relative to
/// `root`, in creation order.
fn write_scaffold(root: &Path, name: &str, pkg_type: &str) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
    let manifest = Manifest {
        package: Package {
            name: name.to_string(),
            version: "0.1.0".to_string(),
            kind: pkg_type.to_string(),
        },
        build: Build::default(),
        dependencies: std::collections::BTreeMap::new(),
    };
    let mut created = Vec::new();
    let carry_toml = root.join("CARRY.toml");
    manifest.save(&carry_toml).with_context(|| {
        format!(
            "writing {}",
            carry_toml.strip_prefix(root).unwrap_or(&carry_toml).display()
        )
    })?;
    created.push("CARRY.toml".to_string());
    for (rel, content) in files_for(name, pkg_type) {
        let path = root.join(&rel);
        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
                .with_context(|| format!("creating {}", parent.display()))?;
        }
        std::fs::write(&path, content)
            .with_context(|| format!("writing {}", path.display()))?;
        created.push(rel);
    }
    Ok(created)
}

fn print_created(created: &[String]) {
    for rel in created {
        println!("  {rel}");
    }
}

/// `carry new <name> [--lib]` — create `./<name>/` with the full scaffold.
pub fn cmd_new(name: &str, lib: bool) -> Result<()> {
    manifest::validate_name(name)?;
    let pkg_type = if lib { "both" } else { "bin" };
    let root = Path::new(name);
    match std::fs::metadata(root) {
        Ok(meta) => {
            if !meta.is_dir() {
                bail!("{name} exists and is not a directory");
            }
            if root.join("CARRY.toml").is_file() {
                bail!("{name} is already a carry project\nhelp: cd into it and run 'carry build'");
            }
            let has_entries = std::fs::read_dir(root)
                .with_context(|| format!("reading {name}"))?
                .any(|entry| entry.is_ok());
            if has_entries {
                bail!("{name} dir exists and is not a carry project\nhelp: remove it or pick a different name");
            }
        }
        Err(err) if err.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {}
        Err(err) => return Err(err).with_context(|| format!("checking {name}")),
    }
    let created = write_scaffold(root, name, pkg_type)
        .with_context(|| format!("creating project in {name}"))?;
    println!("created carry project {name} (type: {pkg_type})");
    print_created(&created);
    println!();
    println!("next: cd {name} && carry build");
    Ok(())
}

/// `carry init` — turn the current directory into a carry project (type "bin",
/// name from the directory name).
pub fn cmd_init() -> Result<()> {
    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().context("reading current directory")?;
    let name = cwd
        .file_name()
        .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
        .unwrap_or("")
        .to_string();
    manifest::validate_name(&name)?;
    if cwd.join("CARRY.toml").is_file() {
        bail!("already a carry project\nhelp: run 'carry build' instead");
    }
    let created = write_scaffold(&cwd, &name, "bin")
        .with_context(|| format!("initializing carry project in {}", cwd.display()))?;
    println!("initialized carry project {name} (type: bin)");
    print_created(&created);
    println!();
    println!("next: carry build");
    Ok(())
}

```
